**This is the ONLY open-work list** (owner, 2026-09-12). Handoff memories, the
audit-bus ledger and agent reports are evidence, not backlogs: anything still
open from them gets a line here, with the check that guards it once one exists.

**Layout (reconciled 2026-09-23, Q16):** (1) the live QUEUE, verbatim, first;
(2) every item still open from older sections, one line each with its current
status and a pointer into the archive; (3) the 2026-09-02 ledger's leftovers
(docs/audit/OPEN_ITEMS.md was retired, then deleted 2026-09-27; history in docs/archive/OPEN_ITEMS-2026-09-02.md); (4) the audit bus, by
reference. History (closed sections, narratives) is verbatim in
[docs/archive/OPEN-history-2026-09.md](archive/OPEN-history-2026-09.md),
with a reconcile log of every item closed and its evidence. Nothing was deleted.

**Done items leave this file (Q16, 2026-09-26):** a ticked `- [x]` item is moved
verbatim to the dated archive docs/archive/OPEN-done-YYYY-MM.md by
`npm run inventories:refresh` (scripts/archive-done.mjs); check:generated fails
while one is left here. The queue score, next free number and the done-item
guard check read this file AND the archives, so archiving never changes a count.

**`[~]` items say when they are done (2026-09-27):** put what is left as one or
more markers on the item, written out in full and ANDed together:
``done-when: sql `SELECT <one column>` => <expected>`` (read-only, first row),
`done-when: test <path>`, `done-when: issue #N closed`, or `done-when: pr #N merged`.
Only add markers when they cover everything that is left (a screenshot or a
review still to do means no marker yet). `node scripts/open-done-when.mjs` runs them;
the nightly `open-done-when` workflow opens one nightly-red issue listing the items
that are ready to tick. src/test/openPartlyDoneItemsSayDoneWhen.test.ts ratchets the
count of `[~]` items with no marker.

**Priority among the open queue items (lead's order, 2026-09-23 — money and
security first, then alerting, then the audit, then hygiene):** Q53 (DB outage

- [ ] **Q7 MEDIUM WebKit only: the bottom nav isn't frosted. OWNER (device), re-checked 2026-09-26: still unmeasured in WebKit.** Nothing code-side can settle it. The dock is signed-in only (guest /browse renders no MobileNav, checked at 375), the cloud session that took this item had no test-account credentials, and Playwright WebKit on Linux is not the iOS compositor, so no CI run can show WKWebView frost either. What the source does (MobileNav.tsx:705-745): the full-width curtain carries `backdropFilter`/`WebkitBackdropFilter: blur(32px) saturate(170%)` together with `maskImage`/`WebkitMaskImage` on the SAME element, and the pill carries `blur(40px) saturate(180%)` inside the nav, whose own style has `transform` + `willChange: transform` (the hide-on-scroll slide). Both are inline styles, so the CSS minifier cannot have dropped the -webkit- form. **Ask:** on the iOS 26.1 simulator or a device, signed in, open /home, scroll the feed under the dock and send one screenshot. **Recommendation:** if the pill is frosted and only the band above it is clear, the curtain's mask is the suspect (test by removing `WebkitMaskImage` alone). If the pill is clear too, test with `willChange` removed from the nav. Fix whichever one screenshot proves, and add a pixel-variance check under the dock to a WebKit spec. (Q1101 merged here 2026-10-03, verify pass: the same work: "LOW WebKit only: bottom nav is not frosted (feed text shows sharp under icons) — Not reached — needs device/s")

5. **Push notifications (Q82): three things only you can do.** The code
   **ANSWERED 2026-09-26 (owner pop-up): "cut it when main is current".** Claude runs fastlane beta from main
   once the open landings are in and main CI is green; you install it and open the app once.
   cause is fixed and pushed (see Q82), but no phone has the fix yet.
   (a) **Cut a TestFlight build from main** (at or after the Q82 fix commit)
   and install it on your iPhone. The only builds with the AppDelegate
   forwarding are ce4d38d59 (2026-09-02) and later, and every build so far
   also has the boot race (it depends on timing, so it may not hit every
   launch). (b) **On that build: sign in, go to Home, tap "Enable" on the
   notifications pill, then Allow.** The pill only shows after you've posted
   or applied, or on a later launch more than an hour after install. If you tapped "Don't
   Allow" on any earlier build, iOS will not ask again: turn it on in
   Settings > Helpr > Notifications first, then cold-launch the app. Within
   seconds `select count(*) from push_tokens` should be 1 and
   analytics_events should show `push_token_saved`. If it doesn't, the
   analytics events say which step failed. (c) **Only if you run the app
   straight from Xcode on your phone:** those builds get SANDBOX APNs tokens,
   and prod sends to the production APNs host (`APNS_USE_SANDBOX` is unset),
   so APNs rejects them with BadDeviceToken and the sender deletes the row.
   TestFlight/App Store builds are unaffected. Leave this alone unless you
   test from Xcode. APNs credentials are fine: APNS_KEY_ID, APNS_TEAM_ID
   (digest matches P85MCK558V), APNS_BUNDLE_ID (digest matches com.Helpr)
   and APNS_AUTH_KEY are all set in Supabase secrets. Whether the .p8 key
   itself is valid can't be checked without a real token to send to.

Message flags are already warn-first (warning, final_warning, then 7-day restriction + admin alert). What still auto-suspends with no human is `auto_restrict_repeat_violators`: on any violation type it does not exclude (it excludes admin_action, admin_warning, cancel_with_helper, off_platform, job_denial, no_show), it counts ALL of the user's violations ever (`COUNT(*)`, no type filter, no time window): 1 = final warning, 2 = 7-day temp_banned, 3 = 30 days. 7. **Turn off Supabase's GitHub auto-deploy to production (Q121). Top priority.**
   On every push to main, Supabase's GitHub integration applies new migrations
   (and deploys edge functions) to prod within about a minute. Our deploy
   workflow's safety checks (lint, replay test, destructive-DDL stop) run after
   that, so for the last 24h (24 migrations) they have not blocked anything.
   Measured from prod's own logs 2026-09-23 (Q117). What you do: Supabase
   dashboard > Project Settings > Integrations > GitHub, and switch off
   automatic deploys to the production branch (exact toggle name not verified).
   Until then, each new migration turns db-deploy red by design (the new
   provenance check), so the side channel stays visible.
   **ANSWERED 2026-09-26 (owner asked "whats the best option?"): switch it OFF.** Measured: db-deploy
   36261725456 (18:16Z) judged 124 prod versions since the cutoff, 108 with a db-deploy receipt and 16
   acknowledged side-channel applies, the newest of them 2026-09-23 ~16:10Z (Q194). So nothing has come
   in by the side channel for about 3 days, but that is not proof the toggle is off. Off is the only
   setting where db-deploy's lint, replay and destructive-DDL checks actually run before prod changes.
   Still the owner's dashboard click; the provenance check stays as the detector either way.

7. **Backups (Q45): the restore is proven weekly now; five things are yours.**
   **ANSWERED 2026-09-23 (owner pop-ups): (a) yes, Pro is paid and intended; (b) keep 14-day retention; (c) back up uploaded files EXCEPT id-documents, encrypted (work item Q147); (d) no PITR (no paid upgrade); (e) BACKUP_PASSPHRASE and ban_fingerprint_salt go in the owner's password manager (owner to-do).**
   Measured 2026-09-23. Nothing below is needed for the drill to stay green.
   (a) **Is the Supabase org really on Pro?** The API says `plan: "pro"`, and
   `supabase backups list` shows 7 daily platform backups. Every doc said
   "free tier". If you are paying $25/mo, you already have in-place daily
   restores for 7 days. If you did not mean to be on Pro, that is a bill.
   (b) **Backup retention is 14 days, not 90.** The repo caps artifacts and
   logs at 14 days (`maximum_allowed_days` 90). Raising it to 90 is one
   setting, but it lengthens EVERY workflow's artifacts, and on the free
   GitHub plan that can go past the artifact-storage quota (billed). The
   alternative is a monthly copy somewhere else. Your call.
   (c) **Storage files (photos, ID documents) are in NO backup**: 152 objects,
   about 10 MB (proof-photos 64, job-photos 61, message-attachments 18,
   user-documents 4, avatars 4, id-documents 1). The platform backup excludes
   them too. Backing them up needs a service-role key as an Actions secret and
   puts ID documents (encrypted) into a GitHub artifact: a privacy trade-off.
   (d) **PITR** (restore to the minute) is a paid add-on, about $100/mo plus
   Small compute. Without it, worst-case loss is about 24 h (platform) or
   24 h + GitHub's up-to-7 h schedule delay (ours). You said no paid upgrades,
   so this is only the measured gap.
   (e) **Two secrets that no backup holds.** `BACKUP_PASSPHRASE`: is there a
   copy outside GitHub? If not, losing the GitHub account also loses every
   backup. `ban_fingerprint_salt` (vault): it cannot be re-created, and
   without it every stored ban fingerprint stops matching after a restore.
   Want it (encrypted) in the backup, or kept in your password manager?

15. **Tax forms (W-9) after a Helpr deletes their account (CS-003, 2026-09-24).**
   **ANSWERED 2026-09-24 (owner pop-up): keep 4 years after signing, then delete automatically. Work item Q370.**
   Deleting an account leaves the Helpr's W-9 record (typed legal name, signing IP)
   in place forever. The IRS expects a business to keep W-9s for about 4 years, so
   deleting them at once may be wrong too. Options: (a) keep them 4 years after
   deletion, then delete automatically; (b) delete them with the account; (c) keep
   forever. I recommend (a), but it is a legal call. No W-9s exist in prod yet.

   then delete them automatically; (b) delete them as soon as the account is
   deleted. I recommend (a).

19. **Delete the Broadcasts feature's code? (S-004, 2026-09-24).** You removed
   **ANSWERED 2026-09-24 (owner pop-up): (a) delete the banner, the admin screen and its menu item now; drop the two tables in a later migration. Work item Q363.**
   Broadcasts on 2026-09-01, but the code is still there: the Dashboard runs two
   database reads for the banner on every load (both tables hold 0 rows), and
   Admin still has a Broadcasts screen that can create banners. My automatic
   permission check stopped me deleting a whole feature without you. Options:
   (a) delete the banner, the admin screen and its menu item now, and drop the
   two tables in a later migration; (b) keep it. I recommend (a).

20. **Paging for Activity lists (PD-002, 2026-09-24).** Posted Jobs and Applied
   **ANSWERED 2026-09-24 (owner pop-up): (a) add "Show older" after 50 per section NOW. Work item Q372.**
   Jobs load every row at once. The largest account today has 202 posted jobs
   (a test account); real accounts are far smaller. Capping the list would hide
   old jobs, so the fix is a "Show older" button (a visible change). Options:
   (a) add "Show older" after 50 per section; (b) leave it until an account
   gets big. I recommend (b) for now; it is not a launch risk.

21. **Louisiana sales tax is $0 on every job (ME-043, 2026-09-02, still true).**
   **ANSWERED 2026-09-24 (owner: "you decide"): lead's decision is to change nothing in code before a CPA answer. The launch checklist carries a CPA review of a Louisiana Stripe Tax registration + taxable categories + service-fee taxability (with MQ24). Work item Q374.**
   Stripe Tax computed $0.00 on an assembly job billed to a Baton Rouge address,
   although the code marks assembly and handyman as taxable. Stripe only charges
   tax in a state where the account has a tax registration, so this is a Stripe
   dashboard setting, not code. Options: (a) add a Louisiana registration in
   Stripe (Tax → Registrations) before launch, after checking with your
   accountant which job types are taxable; (b) collect no sales tax. I cannot
   choose this for you; it is a legal and tax call.
22. **What to call someone who deleted their account (AL-011a, 2026-09-24).**
   **ANSWERED 2026-09-24: same as MQ14 (Q369).**

   neighbor", "Helpr", "Former Helpr", "Name not on file", and in admin
   "Unknown", "User", "Unnamed", "No name", "Deleted user", "Deleted account".
   I fixed the two that were plainly broken (a gift card read "from A"; admin
   referrals showed a raw ID). Pick one word for users and one for admin, e.g.
   users see "a former member", admin sees "Deleted account"?

   **ANSWERED 2026-09-24 (owner pop-up): KEEP the 742 rows. Nothing to do.**

   **ANSWERED 2026-09-24: folded into Q374 (the CPA review on the launch checklist).**
   checkout charges sales tax on the job itself (for taxable categories) but
   NOT on the poster's service fee; the code says "non-taxable until the LA
   Department of Revenue clarifies". In April both were taxed. If the answer is
   "taxable", the platform owes roughly 1.3% of every job budget it did not
   collect. This needs your CPA, not code: when they answer, I switch one line

   **ANSWERED 2026-09-24 (owner pop-up): the lead archives them via the API. Work item Q373.**
   Stripe account had (audit read 2026-09-07; I cannot re-read live Stripe

   month, plus yearly) from the business plans that were removed in August.
   Nothing in the app can sell them, but a checkout on one would take money and

26. **When was the "Sign in with Apple" website secret made? (expiry monitor, 2026-09-24).**

    Apple sign-in on the website uses a secret that Apple lets live at most 6
    months; when it runs out, "Sign in with Apple" on the website stops working
    with no warning. Supabase only shows a scrambled copy of it, so the monitor
    cannot read its date (checked today). Either (a) tell me roughly when you
    made it with tools/apple-jwt.html and I will record its expiry so the
    monitor warns 30 days ahead, or (b) make a fresh one now (you need the .p8
    key from the Apple developer site) and paste it into Supabase > Auth >
    Apple, and I will record today + 6 months.

    does Vercel > Account Settings > Tokens show as its expiration ("No
    Expiration" is a fine answer)?

    (20260926041132, Q225/V-008) both fix "parish alert arrives before early access shows the job", differently:
    (A) KEEP MAIN'S queue-only design (every send goes through the 1-minute sweep, so elite/no-delay users hear up
    to ~60s later; nothing sent inside the funding write) and ADD the PR's block check (`user_blocks`, both ways)
    and shared `job_announceable_to()` gate. A user who deletes the alert can be told again if the job reopens.
    (B) TAKE THE PR'S design: elite/no-delay users told inline at funding (incl. email via net.http_post inside the
    escrow write), a permanent per-(user, job) `job_match_queue` ledger so nobody is ever told twice, block check;
    main's `parish_match_alert_queue` and its sweep step are retired.

    checks `user_blocks` today; `deliver_job_match`/`job_announceable_to` do not exist on prod.

    noindex) are being landed separately. The PR's old-timestamp migrations must never reach main as-is.
    **ANSWERED 2026-09-26 (owner pop-up): "A + never twice": keep main's queue-only send path, add the

    (GROUP_JOBS_ENABLED=false), so nothing is charged under these yet. (A) CREW_FEE_PAYS_UNCONFIRMED:
    a late-cancel fee is shared by a member the poster OFFERED the job to but who never confirmed.
    Built as yes (your Q407(13) answer); confirm. (B) CREW_COMPLETES_WHEN_HIRED_DONE: an under-filled
    crew completes when every hired member is done, and the unfilled shares go back to the poster.
    Built as yes; confirm. (C) New: when a crew dispute or an unfilled slot returns a member's share,
    only the share itself (budget + urgent fee) goes back. The platform fee, sales tax and any gift
    card on that charge are NOT returned. Should they be returned in proportion to the share?
    ANSWERED 2026-09-27: yes, pro-rata. BUILT (see the ANSWERED block above); guard src/test/edge/process-scheduled-payouts-crew.test.ts.

Only tests that read `fx.openJob` should skip; they already carry per-test `test.skip(!fx.openJob, …)`. The other 2 skips (expanding-search) are justified. Fix: let the fixture return "none" in live mode instead of skipping, keep the live-pay skip per test, and GUARD the class: a `beforeAll` must not call a fixture that can `test.skip` (static scan of e2e/prod-audit, @mutate one spec back). Then re-measure `prod-audit.perTest`: set to 138.2 (the max of runs 36744778439 = 138.2 and 36766443019 = 137.7, both with 91 tests run) so the budget step passes today. Run 36364639347 (before the switch, 124 tests) measured 109.9, which is inside the two-way band, so the un-skip will not trip it; lower it to the new measurement in the same commit. done-when: issue #1754 closed (tick when a prod-audit run shows only justified skips, and `prod-audit.perTest` equals its measured value). **Fixed 2026-09-30 (Q865 lane):** the three beforeAll hooks now mint inside `unlessLivePay(() => ...)` (e2e/prod-audit/fundedOpenJob.ts), which turns the live-pay skip into a value; only tests that open a funded fixture skip, per test, via `skipWhenUnfundedLive` -> `skipLivePay`. Guard: `src/test/journeysNeverPayLive.test.ts` "no beforeAll mints a funded fixture outside unlessLivePay" (2 @mutate lines, both killed). **perTest NOT changed:** the un-skipped tests now run, so `prod-audit.perTest` must be RE-MEASURED from the next real prod-audit run and set to that value (not 138.2, which was measured with 91 tests). Three layers, from the logs of runs 5-9 (36055191338, 36069105982, 36158775025, 36165226511, 36186004139): (1) request budget over the 400/min ceiling on main (752 and 733/min, runs 7 and 9): FIXED by porting ed86c0647 from PR #1797 (paceTo + meteredRunsArePaced guard); (2) real skeleton breaches on /jobs, /posts, /user/:id: NOT carried here; #1797 owns them (its 43e716d59 was ported, then reverted because e2e/prod-audit/activity-loading-reserve.spec.ts `jobs` pitch is red before mutation on it, vacuity run 36170345228), so they stay baselined debt under Q201 until #1797 lands; (3) THE MEASUREMENT DID NOT REPEAT: runs 7 and 9 on the SAME commit 3569359 disagreed on 21 of 139 surface lines (78 vs 73 measured; `helper /profile` cl=2/2 vs no-placeholder), so the two-way baseline churned (stale entries differ run to run). ROOT CAUSE: one browser context per persona was reused across every surface while the app persists React Query to IndexedDB (src/lib/queryPersister.ts), so a surface visited after a sibling that fetched the same queries rehydrated and drew no skeleton (or a subset), in an order set by two racing workers. FIXED: measure-loading-states.mjs opens a fresh context per surface and closes it. GUARD: src/test/loadingStatesColdContext.test.ts (red on the pre-fix file; 4 @mutate each red; fixture shows per-persona and per-viewport sharing rejected). Also: check-staleness.mjs counted a green run on ANY branch (`gh run list` had no --branch) and a branch dispatch's `land`/`notify` would land a branch measurement and open/close main's nightly-red issue; now WORKFLOW_BOUND entries carry `branch: "main"` (guard: stalenessWatch.test.ts, 2 new @mutate red) and land/notify run on refs/heads/main only. CONFIRMED CAUSE (2026-09-26): cold runs 36201048187 and 36202873061 measured 38 and 41 of 139 with DIFFERENT sets, and every miss read 'no loading placeholder at any settled stage (0 wave(s) released)': nextStage returned 'empty' on the blank BOOT frame, in the gap between the last chunk landing and the app's first query (screen unchanged 600ms, nothing held, nothing in flight). Reproduced locally on anon /browse at 6x CPU throttle: old logic 0/6 measured, fixed logic 6/6 (unthrottled 3/3; /login, /help, 404 still end 'empty'). FIX: 'empty' needs the app booted (visible text in #root; the route fallback's sr-only 'Loading…' does not count) and the WIRE quiet as well as the screen. GUARD: src/test/loadingStatesRepeat.test.ts boot-frame case + @mutate (red with the rule removed). The fresh context per surface is kept as a second, order-dependent source removed; its IndexedDB effect was not separately shown. The log line now prints each no-placeholder reason, redirect and the surface's backend request count. FIX: on 'won', when no clawback was repaid and the ONE thing holding a 'chargeback' job is a decided single-Helpr split that has not run, the payment state the decision left is restored (preDecisionPaymentStatus: payout_pending only with a schedule, else escrow), a compare-and-set on status + payment_status + the dispute_status read, KEEPING disputed_at, so an admin can Retry settlement and no automatic payout can; ANY other hold (an open dispute or a 'disputed' job, a crew decision, a legacy group decision, a settlement claim, a reversed payout) keeps the job 'chargeback' and pages CRITICAL 'settle it by hand' with the manual step (no admin tool can settle a 'chargeback' job) (each has an automatic settler the restore would switch back on: lh-money-escrow review HIGH). The hold read fails closed on 'won' too; the payment notices no longer fire on a job leaving 'chargeback' (20261003184911, Q805 (2)). GUARD: src/test/decidedDisputeReachesTerminal.test.ts (won = restores, gated on decidedSplitOnly) and src/test/edge/disputeSettlementWebhook.test.ts (Q449 block, 12 cases, red on main's handler); every @mutate killed by hand. Sensitive-Review lh-money-escrow on the commit. LEFT: confirm in Stripe TEST mode that a charge whose card dispute was WON can still be refunded (the split's refund leg runs on exactly that charge, after its transfer leg); do it in the same test-mode run as Q805 (1). Until then the admin's 'Chargeback WON' notice says to check that in Stripe before Retry settlement. Was: chargeDisputeClosed 'won' leaves the job payment_status='chargeback' unless a clawback was repaid; execute-dispute-split refuses 'chargeback', so the decided split cannot run and admins are only told "job still on hold". Needs: restore the pre-chargeback payment state on 'won' when an unexecuted decided dispute holds the job (as warning_closed does), after confirming in Stripe test mode that a charge whose dispute was won can still be refunded by the split. - [ ] **Q444 HIGH A group job can be marked released while a roster member is unpaid (lh-money-escrow review of Q355 part 2, 2026-09-26, code read only, not reproduced).** supabase/functions/stripe-webhook/handlers/transferCreated.ts flips jobs.payment_status to 'released' for ANY transfer that has a payout_transfers row, whenever the job is payout_pending/escrow/released, with no roster check. process-scheduled-payouts deliberately keeps a group job payout_pending until every roster member has a pending/paid payout_transfers row (the allRosterPaid block). So when one member's transfer throws ('Scheduled payout failed'), another member's transfer.created webhook can release the job; the cron selects payment_status = 'payout_pending' and never retries the unpaid member. Q355 part 2's money-held rule counts that state as still owed, so the alert stays open, but the payout itself is stuck. Fix: give transferCreated the same roster test (only the transfer that completes the roster may flip a group job), with a PGlite/edge test red on the current handler. Needs a money-escrow review.

The lead researches Louisiana law from public sources (not professional tax advice) and decides (1)-(3). LEAD DECIDED 2026-09-27 (from public sources: R.S. 47:301.3, 47:340.1, the LDR reform FAQ, RSIB 23-001; this is not professional tax advice): (1) KEEP the Louisiana registration in Stripe Tax. Check that live taxreg_1TAZVsKp2H4b7tECyvJINQyg still covers state and local tax. ADD the same registration in TEST mode (the likely cause of Q441's $0), re-run Q441, and record the tax in cents. Ask LDR or the Remote Sellers Commission whether LH files as a marketplace facilitator (one return) or with LDR plus each parish, before the first taxable sale. (2) Taxable categories: assembly (fabrication or repair of tangible personal property) and handyman stay taxable. Handyman over-collects on real-property work, which is the safer direction. All other categories stay exempt. Follow-up build: add a 'work on a movable item?' flag so handyman on real property is exempt and carpet, rug and upholstery cleaning is taxed. Also fix the salesTax.ts header ('ten services' should be 11). (3) Service fee: CONSERVATIVE. On a TAXABLE job, the fee takes the labor line's tax code (create-payment ~640-650, plus the recurring precompute ~1446-1469). It stays nontaxable on exempt jobs. Tips stay nontaxable. This is a money-path build, reviewed with lh-money-escrow and stripeTaxCodes.test.ts updated.

The poster presses Message Helpr, which goes to `/messages?jobId=<job>&userId=<helper>` (confirmed in the trace); openConvo fetched the thread's messages, but the page ended on the list at /messages?chat=1 with no reply box. The same deep link opens the thread with a reply box for helpr-e2e-poster-0902 on prod at normal speed (checked 2026-09-24). Evidence: the run's artifacts and trace, copied to the session scratchpad. - [ ] **Q390** (MEDIUM, OA-004, 2026-09-25): OWNER QUESTION (2026-09-26): move the native session mirror from @capacitor/preferences (NSUserDefaults plist, in device backups) to the iOS Keychain / Android Keystore (kSecAttrAccessibleAfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnly)? Recommendation: yes, before launch, in a native release; it needs a new plugin in the .ipa/.apk, so it ships only with a build, and a real-device check (relaunch, Offload App, WebKit localStorage eviction) that cannot run in a cloud session. Nothing code-side was changed here: switching the web code to a plugin that installed builds do not carry would log those users out. Original text: the native Supabase session (access + refresh token) is stored in NSUserDefaults via @capacitor/preferences (an unencrypted plist, in device backups, not passcode-gated) and in WKWebView localStorage. The misleading `keychainStorageAdapter` name is fixed (now `preferencesStorageAdapter`; guard `src/test/secureStorageNamesAreTrue.test.ts`). Open: owner decision whether to move the native mirror to a Keychain-backed plugin (kSecAttrAccessibleAfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnly); needs a device check that sessions still survive relaunch, Offload App and WebKit localStorage eviction. No guard yet for the storage choice itself.

OWNER STEPS: (1) Stripe Dashboard > Tax > Registrations, in BOTH Test mode and Live mode: confirm an ACTIVE United States / Louisiana registration, add it if missing (ME-019 recorded one ACTIVE in live on 2026-09-02, taxreg_1TAZVsKp2H4b7tECyvJINQyg; test mode is unknown); (2) give the CPA the three Q374 questions plus two more: should taxable labor use a repair/maintenance-of-tangible-personal-property code instead of "General - Services" (the answer is one line, `TAXABLE_LABOR_TAX_CODE`), and is tax owed on the part of an assembly/handyman job paid by gift card (create-payment prices a gift shortfall line Nontaxable and a fully gift-paid job charges nothing). (3) Dashboard > Products: the Pro membership prices are dashboard Price objects, so their tax code is set there, not in code; check it. After (1), one test-mode assembly checkout at an LA address should show Tax > $0; if it still shows $0, the code is the cause. feed: bus ME-019. done-when: bus ME-019 closed feed: bus ME-043. done-when: bus ME-043 closed
- [ ] **Q446 MEDIUM** (OWNER/DEVICE, OA-018 identity linking, 2026-09-25; supersedes Q359's steps): what an existing email account gets when it signs in with Apple/Google is now settled from GoTrue's source (supabase/auth linking.go + external.go, read 2026-09-25) and checked on our DB layer by `scripts/check-identity-linking.mjs` (db-smoke.yml on every migration PR, db-drift-detect.yml nightly on prod, rolled back): verified same email -> the SAME account (no second profile, profile data kept); UNverified email account -> the same account, GoTrue wipes its password and confirms the email (profile kept); Apple "Hide My Email" -> a NEW account that lands on /complete-profile; an unverified provider email -> refused with provider_email_needs_verification, now explained on /login (was "That page needs an account": ~/.lh-shots/oa018/before-375.png vs after-375.png). What no script can do is a real consent screen. Steps, each on web (Chrome) AND the iOS TestFlight build, with an address you control: (1) sign up with email+password, verify it, post one job; sign out; tap Continue with Google with the same Gmail -> you must land in the SAME account (the job is there; admin: `select user_id, provider from auth.identities where user_id = '<id>'` shows email + google). (2) Same with Sign in with Apple choosing "Share My Email" -> same account. (3) Sign in with Apple choosing "Hide My Email" -> a NEW account on the Complete your profile form (expected; see Q447). (4) Sign up with email+password and do NOT click the verify link; then Continue with Google on that address -> you are signed in to that account, and the password you set no longer works (Forgot Password restores it; see Q447). Record user ids and screenshots in the OA-018 note. Close with the four results, not before. (Q359 merged here 2026-10-03, verify pass: the same work: "Needs a real Google account: sign up with email, sign out, then "Continue with Google" with the same address,")
- [ ] **Q447 MEDIUM** (OWNER DECISION, OA-018 follow-ups, 2026-09-25, from GoTrue's source, not reproduced on a device): (a) PRE-ACCOUNT DATA, **MEDIUM** (rated by the lh-authz-rls review of #1806, 2026-09-26): when an UNverified email account is linked (Q446 step 4), GoTrue removes its password and email identity to stop a pre-account takeover, but OUR profile row (name, phone, DOB, photo, city, bio, marketing consent, legal acceptances, referral) is kept as whoever typed it at signup. Wider than first listed (code read 2026-09-26): complete-signup's unauthenticated path (supabase/functions/complete-signup/index.ts ~200-269: account under 30 min old, never signed in, empty bio) also takes the avatar, license and insurance uploads, writes legal_acceptances and records the referral, and because it fills every ProtectedRoute gate field the victim never passes /complete-profile to see it; the attacker's phone becomes the contact shown to counterparties. GoTrue leaves the attacker no session, refresh token, MFA factor or phone identity (token.go refuses the password grant to an unconfirmed email; signup refuses email+phone together), and cleanup-abandoned-accounts deletes unconfirmed accounts after 30 days. Referral attribution should also require a confirmed email. If an attacker signed up first with a victim's address, the victim's Google sign-in lands in an account whose profile the attacker wrote. Options: keep (today), or clear the signup-entered profile fields when email_confirmed_at is first set by a provider link (no email identity left), sending the person back through /complete-profile. Clearing also costs a legitimate user who signed up and then used Google instead of the email link. (b) APPLE HIDE-MY-EMAIL makes a second account for someone who already has one; /complete-profile could say "Already have a Helpr account under another email? Sign out and log in with it" when the address is @privaterelay.appleid.com. Guard to name when done: a case in scripts/sql/identity-linking-scenarios.sql for (a); a /complete-profile test for (b). **OWNER DECIDED 2026-10-04 (pop-up): when the real email owner takes over a never-verified account, everything typed before verification is DELETED (profile fields, uploads and their storage objects, consents, legal acceptances, referral); the owner fills in their own profile. Being built by the security lane.**

Messages.tsx also runs the check on realtime recovery, so a deletion during an outage is caught (no event is left behind). If it does not, the reconnect check and Q334's refused send still flip it. GUARD: src/pages/messages/openThreadFlipsWhenCounterpartyDeleted.test.tsx (hook raises the hint only for that event; page flips only the named open thread; 6 @mutate lines shown red; red on origin/main) + messagesReceiverNullable (flip is the shared function). **STATUS 2026-10-04 (lead): landed in the 2026-10-04 batch 2, reviewed; tick after its deploy is verified live.**
- [~] **Q511 MEDIUM** (lh-authz-rls review of Q335, 2026-09-26; pre-existing, not introduced by Q335): a Restore on one device was undone by another — unarchiveConversation deleted the server row, but a second device's local mirror still held the key and its next loadArchives merge-up pushed it back, hiding the thread everywhere. Fixed 2026-09-26 (owner: "fix Q511 too", branch cloud/open-messaging-deleted): archivedConversations keeps a per-user PENDING set (safeStorage `helpr_archived_conversations_pending_<uid>`) of archives this device made and never saw confirmed; archiveConversation marks pending and clears on a confirmed write (or rollback), unarchiveConversation clears it, and the merge-up (pure `planMergeUp`) pushes only pending keys and DROPS a local-only key the server confirmed once and no longer has. Upgrade note: a device's first load after this ships has no pending set, so any archive it made offline and never synced is dropped once (the thread reappears and can be archived again) — chosen over re-pushing, which is the bug. Guard: src/test/mirrorMergeUpPushesOnlyPending.test.ts (class = every src/lib module with a safeStorage mirror and an .upsert merge-up, derived from source; KNOWN_PUSHES_ALL exact both ways; 4 @mutate lines, each shown red). Left: live two-device check (restore on device B, reload device A, thread stays in the inbox) — not possible from this session (no test-account credentials). STATUS 2026-09-27 (lane P3): needs the live two-device check; not run. Stays [~]. STATUS 2026-10-02 (lane D): retired_client_relations stale_reads sum = 0; the cross-device Restore still needs a two-device browser check.

The breach is ONE page burst, not pacing: `/jobs/6e1a1c36-3d62-4b7d-8eea-fdbd233d52c2` (job-detail-completed) sent 330 requests on webkit (194 chromium) between two gates, more than the whole 200 per-worker share, so no gate can bound it. AVATAR CLASS (2026-09-27): the run's top repeated GET, `/storage/v1/object/public/avatars/:id/avatar.jpg` x719, was an APP defect, not the meter. UserAvatar set `src` before `crossOrigin` (React applies props in order), so WebKit/WKWebView started a no-cors load, then a second CORS load, and shared neither with the next <img> of the same URL (cache headers are fine: GET max-age=3600). Measured in Playwright WebKit, 40 <img> of one URL: src-first 41 requests, crossOrigin-first 1 (Chromium 1 either way). On a local build against prod /posts?filter=done, one Helpr avatar went 101 -> 1 request, all storage requests 95 -> 42 (evidence ~/.lh-shots/avatar-meter/). FIX: src/components/UserAvatar.tsx sets crossOrigin/loading before src. Guard src/test/imgCrossOriginBeforeSrc.test.tsx (every JSX <img> in src/, floor >30; plus rendered attribute order): red on origin/main and under its @mutate, green on the fix. - [ ] **Q750 HIGH Q210(b) deferred money edges (lh-money-escrow review, 2026-09-27).** (1) A paid visit that later falls into a skip branch stays held until the visit-date sweep refunds it; (2) refunding a future-dated visit re-parks it; (3) Stripe's answer to a retried refund after "already refunded" is unverified; (4) end_recurring_series / parent job cancel do not expire the open Checkout (a late payment is refunded by webhook or sweep); (5) a Stripe session created but not stored is orphaned. Done when each is fixed or recorded as accepted, with a test. PROGRESS 2026-10-03 (edge tests red on main: 10 failed, all three files green after): (3) FIXED: Stripe documents `charge_already_refunded` for a refund of a charge with nothing left (docs.stripe.com/error-codes, read 2026-10-03; a key replays only within 24h); on main the webhook threw (critical "refund failed" page + 500, so Stripe redelivered) and the cron's createRefundOnce did the same. Both now count it, like an idempotency conflict, as done only when the intent carries a live refund (stripe-webhook.test.ts + charge-recurring-visits.test.ts "Q750 (3)"). (4) FIXED: on main a payment on a Checkout left open after the series ended or its parent was cancelled was marked PAID, held, and returned on the visit date LESS the card fee; the webhook now reads the parent and refunds it in full and expires the row (500 on an unreadable parent), and the daily sweep expires pending rows of an over series and closes their Checkout (stripe-webhook.test.ts / charge-recurring-visits.test.ts "Q750 (4)"). (5) FIXED: a session whose row stopped waiting before it was stored (zero rows) is expired at once; on a DB error it is left open on purpose so the same-key retry stores it (create-payment.test.ts "Q750 (5)"). (1) FIXED for a series that is OVER (ended or parent cancelled): the sweep reads the parent of every open future row (paged to the end), those parents' end state, then only the over parents' open rows (so its 500-row cap counts only those, lh-money-escrow review 2026-10-03), and refunds a paid one now under the Q808 cause rules; a paid row already booked on its intent is marked funded, never refunded ("Q750 (1)" and "Q750 (1) review" tests, plus a control that a live, disputed or unread series' rows are never read). Review follow-ups filed as Q1221-Q1224. STILL OPEN, OWNER DECISION: (1) for a PAUSED series (a ban or block on the Helpr, a date nobody holds, a dispute, a chargeback stop) the paid visit stays held until its date by design because it may still be booked; recommendation: keep holding it (≤ FUND_LEAD_DAYS = 3 days), since refunding early is what (2) turns into a second payment. (2) a series that is over never re-parks (asserted in "Q750 (1)"); a still-live, still-held date whose paid visit was refunded in the loop (insert refused, or the date changed hands mid-run, fee withheld per Q808) IS re-parked and the payer asked to pay again, the on-session twin of the off-session re-charge on a new hold id (20260927012806). Owner: accept that (recommended), or book the refused visit on the paid intent with the new holder instead of refunding.

- [ ] **Q1159 MEDIUM Clients can mute server pages through error_logs (found 2026-10-03 by the lh-authz-rls review of Q1156).** Measured by the lead: anon holds INSERT on public.error_logs (has_table_privilege, prod 2026-10-03). Reported by the review from the live function bodies (not yet re-run): notify_slack_on_error_log's per-source, per-severity throttle and sweep_dead_crons' once-a-day 'cron-dead' dedupe both count client-written rows, and stamp_error_log_origin re-sources only 4 critical sources, so one forged row tagged with a server source (e.g. the hourly 'error' window) suppresses that source's Slack page (the ledger row still lands). Q1156 avoids it with severity fatal, which clients cannot write. Fix: throttles and dedupes count only server-origin rows (origin = 'server'), or clients cannot write server source tags; GUARD: a PGlite proof that a client row no longer mutes a server page, red first. **FIXED 2026-10-04 (cloud branch cloud/q1159-q1174; not merged or deployed yet; an lh-authz-rls review of the migration is required before landing).** Read live first: has_table_privilege anon/authenticated INSERT on error_logs = true; 3,081 pre-stamp rows have no tags.origin, 341 client, 764 server. The class is wider than the two named functions: 15 functions read error_logs to throttle, dedupe or take a receipt by tags the client can forge (notify_slack_on_error_log, sweep_dead_crons, sweep_silent_cron_failures, sweep_cron_http_failures, log_cron_defect, sweep_cron_blackouts, check_ops_digest_delivery (a forged 'ops-digest' row counted as a delivery receipt), check_push_token_health, check_db_saturation, check_error_log_throttle, check_seed_boundary_failures, check_ops_alert_pending, sweep_cron_startup_failures, sweep_email_dlqs (a forged high-water row hid every dead letter below it), sweep_disputes_closed_without_payment). supabase/migrations/20261004004835_client_rows_cannot_mute_server_alerts.sql adds `coalesce(tags ->> 'origin', '') <> 'client'` to each read (bodies byte-equal to prod prosrc before the edit; `<> 'client'` not `= 'server'` so the 3,081 pre-stamp rows still count). GUARD: src/test/pglite/clientRowsCannotMuteServerAlerts.pglite.mjs (client forgery through the real stamp + RLS policy: red on the chain before the migration, 7 FAILED; green after, applied 3x; MUTATE=no-origin-filter 7 FAILED) and src/test/errorLogDedupesIgnoreClientRows.test.ts (every function that reads error_logs filters origin or is on an exact two-way list; red without the migration; both @mutate registrations red). Not fixed, filed as Q1208: detect_stuck_payments' seed-digest dedupe (live prosrc differs from its newest migration). **STATUS 2026-10-04 (lead): landed in the 2026-10-04 batch 2, reviewed; tick after its deploy is verified live.**
- [ ] **Q1165 MEDIUM A Helpr's answer to a withdrawn dispute survives a re-file, so they cannot answer the new complaint (found by the lh-authz-rls review of Q966, 2026-10-03; pre-existing, 0 live rows).** No function clears jobs.dispute_helper_response: rpc_withdraw_dispute does not, and neither does open_dispute_as's new-dispute UPDATE (live pg_get_functiondef: only open_dispute_as, settle_one_off_jobs_for_banned_account and the Helpr allow-list mention either dispute text). After withdraw then re-file (the "ABA" flow auto-resolve-disputes documents), the old answer renders under the new complaint, and since 20261003180355 the answer is write-once, so the Helpr can never answer the new one (the card already hid the box once an answer existed, so no UI regression). Fix: clear dispute_helper_response in open_dispute_as's new-dispute UPDATE (SECURITY DEFINER, so the table-door trigger does not apply), with an lh-money-escrow review. GUARD when built: a PGlite probe (open, answer, withdraw, re-file: the answer is NULL and the Helpr's next answer lands). FIXED 2026-10-04 (cloud/q1165-q925, 20261004004705): open_dispute_as's new-dispute UPDATE sets dispute_helper_response = NULL (the evidence-append branch for a still-open dispute keeps it). GUARD: src/test/pglite/refileClearsHelprAnswer.pglite.mjs (open, answer, withdraw, re-file: answer NULL, next answer lands; RED with NEW_MIGRATION=skip) + src/test/refileClearsHelprAnswer.test.ts (@mutate). Needs lh-money-escrow and lh-authz-rls reviews. **STATUS 2026-10-04 (lead): landed in the 2026-10-04 batch 2, reviewed; tick after its deploy is verified live.**
- [ ] **Q1168 LOW public.messages carries a realtime-topic SELECT policy that ignores the row (found by the lh-authz-rls review of Q340, 2026-10-03).** "Users can subscribe to own channels" (FOR SELECT TO authenticated) starts with realtime.topic() ~ ('^(notifications|messages):' || uid), which does not look at the message at all. Inert today (PostgREST never sets realtime.topic; realtime.apply_rls sets only the role and request.jwt.claims, read live), but any session that set that setting would read every message. It looks like a realtime.messages policy created on the wrong table. Fix: confirm no realtime channel join depends on it, then drop it from public.messages (and remove POSTER_SIDE_EXEMPT.messages in src/test/helpers/dataExportInventory.ts, which its two-way check will demand). GUARD when built: a check that no SELECT policy on a public table is satisfiable without referencing the row. STATUS 2026-10-03 (lane lead/messages-guards; NOT fixed there): read live, realtime.messages carries a policy of the same name with the same qual (the copy private channel joins use), so this one reads as a duplicate on the wrong table; POSTER_SIDE_EXEMPT no longer exists anywhere in src (git grep), so no two-way demand waits on it. Left out of 20261004001242 because the guard named here needs a reviewed allowlist (admin-only and public-read SELECT policies are row-blind by design), so it is not a trivial same-migration drop.
- [ ] **Q1170 MEDIUM A returning visitor's token refresh runs in series before every read on the first screen (found by the Q1158 lab work, 2026-10-03).** supabase-js refreshes an expired access token only once the app bundle has downloaded, and every read waits for it. Measured (local build, prod data, poster, cold, Chromium, scripts/perf/cwv-lab.mjs --expired, 375 Slow 4G + 4x CPU): /home `auth/v1/token?grant_type=refresh_token` 4485-5310 ms, first read at 5357, LCP 6396 -> 6996 ms (+600); /jobs refresh 3899-4876, LCP 4924 -> 5672 (+750); at 1440 +~200 ms. A real returning visitor's token is always expired after an hour away, so this is the common real-user load, not a corner. Fix to design: start the refresh from src/entry.ts beside the JS download and hand the result to supabase-js through an async storage getItem (never a second refresh with an already-used token); web only, native keeps its Preferences storage. AUTH change: needs an lh-authz-rls review before it lands. Done when the --expired lab run matches the fresh-token one, with a budget for it in e2e/web-vitals/first-screens.spec.ts.
- [ ] **Q1171 MEDIUM Signed-in first screens ask for their data only after the whole app has downloaded (found by the Q1158 lab work, 2026-10-03).** Measured (local build, prod data, poster, cold, 375 Slow 4G + 4x CPU, scripts/perf/cwv-lab.mjs): the app first draws at 4.37 s on /home and the first Supabase read starts at 4.39 s; the feed lands at ~5.2 s, LCP 5.95 s. /jobs: first read 3.80 s, LCP 4.77 s. The guest /browse already starts its job list from the entry beside the JS (src/boot/guestJobsPrefetch.ts, Q206: data-start 3796 -> 589 ms); the signed-in screens (profile row + the page's primary read) have no such path. Fix: the same pattern for the signed-in first screens, URL held equal to supabase-js's by a test (as src/test/guestJobsPrefetch.test.ts does). Done when the lab LCP of /home and /jobs at 375 drops by the data wait and the KNOWN ceilings in e2e/web-vitals/first-screens.spec.ts are lowered.
- [ ] **Q1172 MEDIUM framer-motion rides two first paints: the dock arrives ~1 s after the page, and /home carries it before its first draw (found by the Q1158 lab work, 2026-10-03).** (1) MobileNav statically imports NavQuickMenu and SharedLayoutPill (framer-motion), so the lazy dock chunk waits on proxy.js (35 KB brotli): filmstrip of a cold /home at 375, Slow 4G + 4x CPU (~/.lh-shots/perf/film1-home-strip.png): title bar and skeleton at 4.4 s, dock at 5.4 s. (2) /home's route closure carries framer through BrowseTasksFeed -> SwipeableJobCard: proxy.js + animate.js (~40 KB brotli) download before its first draw (cwv-lab jsBeforeLcp). Fix: load framer on demand (the useFramerMotion pattern NotificationPanel uses, or LazyMotion), then start the dock's chunk with the page so it draws in the first frame; extend scripts/check-deferred-vendors.mjs from the boot graph to every first screen's route closure, red on today's /home first. Same paint-timing class as Q1158's page fades: OptimizedImage's `fadeIn` starts at opacity 0, which Chrome's LCP does not count, for job photos once the marketplace has them (not measurable on an empty marketplace).
- [ ] **Q1173 LOW /profile's first frame is bones only, so its FCP waits for the profile row (found by the Q1158 lab work, 2026-10-03).** Measured (local build, prod data, poster, cold, 375 Slow 4G + 4x CPU, filmstrip ~/.lh-shots/perf/film2-profile-strip.png): skeleton on screen at 4.16 s, FCP 4.96 s when the profile card landed; LCP is the avatar image (5.72 s, load delay 4.94 s behind the profile read). The menu rows' labels (Schedule, Availability, Saved Helprs, ...) and section names are known before any data; a data-aware skeleton (the owner's Q722 rule) drawing them makes the first frame contentful. Same at 1440 on /jobs and /posts, where the title is sr-only and the header row draws bones (FCP 0.22-0.26 s after the first draw). In e2e/web-vitals/first-screens.spec.ts the mobile /profile FCP is a KNOWN ceiling until this lands.
- [ ] **Q1174 LOW The service worker caches signed-in Supabase responses (found by the Q1158 lab work, 2026-10-03).** vite.config.ts runtimeCaching routes every `https://*.supabase.co/*` GET through Workbox NetworkFirst into Cache Storage `api-cache` (50 entries, 300 s): the signed-in user's rows (profile, applications, messages) are written to disk on every load and every read is proxied through the SW. Sign-out (src/lib/authSignOut.ts) wipes the React Query memory and IndexedDB caches and the proof-photo sign cache, but not Cache Storage, so those responses stay on disk until Workbox expires them (read in source 2026-10-03; not run on a device). Not a measured slowdown (the lab blocks service workers); a privacy question first. Needs an lh-authz-rls look: drop the API route (NetworkOnly) or scope it to anon reads, and clear `api-cache` on sign-out. **2026-10-03 (lead; the same cache found from the e2e side by the Q1164 lane, filed there as a duplicate and folded in here):** 50 entries, 300 s, and nobody has checked that sign-out clears it, so a shared device could show the previous account's data offline. Check the sign-out path (WebKit and Chromium), fix if it survives, guard. **FIXED 2026-10-04 (cloud branch cloud/q1159-q1174; not merged or deployed yet).** vite.config.ts no longer has a rule for https://*.supabase.co/* (a request no rule matches goes straight to the network and is never stored), so the route is dropped rather than scoped; src/lib/apiCachePurge.ts deletes the legacy `api-cache` at sign-out (src/lib/authSignOut.ts, before the calls that can throw) and once per production page load (src/main.tsx), because Workbox only evicts when a rule writes, so devices that already hold it would keep it forever. public/sw.js (Capacitor shell worker) already passes everything else to the network. Not run on a device or in WebKit/Chromium: verified by source, by behaviour tests with a stubbed Cache Storage, and by the emitted dist/sw.js (see the PR). GUARD: src/test/swRuntimeCachingNeverStoresApi.test.ts (pins the whole runtimeCaching list, two-way; red on main's vite.config.ts, 3 failed; all 3 @mutate registrations red) and src/lib/authSignOut.test.ts (sign-out deletes api-cache; red on main's authSignOut.ts, 2 failed). **STATUS 2026-10-04 (lead): landed in the 2026-10-04 batch 2, reviewed; tick after its deploy is verified live.**

- [ ] **Q1186 MEDIUM A pending accept completes only when something asks Stripe for the Helpr's status (lh-authz-rls review of Q1180, #4).** Prod's webhook receives no Connect events (0 account.* rows in stripe_webhook_events since the oldest retained row, 2026-09-04; Q876), so stripe_payouts_enabled only reaches true through stripe-connect `status`, which the app calls when the Helpr opens Profile or Activity. A Helpr who finishes Stripe and does not come back before the offer's deadline loses the offer (no strike). Fix: a scheduled re-check (service role) of every helper_id in job_accept_pending, running stripe-connect's status write-back; or the Connect endpoint (Q876, owner). The lh-money-escrow review (clean with notes) found the third writer of the class, rpc_settle_dispute_without_payment ('closed by an admin, no payment on file'), also unexplained under "Settled: $0.00 · $0.00": it now reads "Closed by an admin: no payment was on file for this job, so nothing was split here." with the $0 line hidden like a chargeback. - [ ] **Q1192 MEDIUM Quick Release and Quick Refund can overwrite a chargeback block that lands during their Stripe call (found by the lh-money-escrow re-review of Q449, 2026-10-03; code read only, NOT exercised).** supabase/functions/create-payment/index.ts admin_release_dispute flips the job to completed/released with .eq("status", "disputed") only (about line 2291), and admin_refund_dispute flips it to cancelled/refunded the same way. If charge.dispute.created sets payment_status 'chargeback' while the transfer (or refund) call is in flight, the flip writes 'released' (or 'refunded') over it: the created handler had BLOCKED the job instead of clawing back the transfer that just left, so a lost chargeback becomes a platform loss and the chargeback marker no longer holds the job. Fix: pin both flips on the payment_status read too (escrow/payout_pending), and on zero rows re-read and, when it is 'chargeback', page and run the clawback path rather than writing over it. Guard to add: an edge case per flip in src/test/edge/create-payment.test.ts asserting the payment_status filter, red on today's flips. **STATUS 2026-10-04 (lead):** built on cloud/q1192-q1193; lh-money-escrow review CHANGES REQUIRED (Quick Release's pin broke on our own transfer.created webhook; the full-refund ledger row recorded the running total); both fixed by the lead (release pins escrow/payout_pending/released, the row records the refund's own amount), merged onto main's Q454 gift restore. Left: re-review, land, deploy, then the done-when.
- [ ] **Q1193 MEDIUM charge.refunded reads charge.refunds, which a webhook's minimal Charge does not carry (found by the lh-money-escrow review of Q450, 2026-10-03; pre-existing, NOT verified live).** supabase/functions/stripe-webhook/handlers/chargeRefunded.ts:48 takes the refund from charge.refunds?.data?.[0]. Since API version 2022-11-15 a Charge no longer carries refunds, and webhook objects are never expanded (Stripe docs: 'Expansion with webhooks'), so if the live endpoint is on a newer version latestRefund is always undefined: a Dashboard refund then writes NO payment_refunds ledger row (full: a logged WARN; partial: silent) and an onboarding-fee correction is never recognised. Measured 2026-10-03: payment_refunds holds 128 rows (admin_refund_dispute 1, admin_refund_general 12, cancel_escrow 110, void_cancelled_payments 5) and 0 'stripe_dashboard' rows ever; the last 24h of logs hold no charge.refunded delivery. Not measured: the live endpoint's API version (stripe-webhook-config-check returns only {id, url, status, livemode, enabled_events}; adding api_version there answers it with no new Stripe call). Fix when confirmed: list the charge's refunds (stripe.refunds.list({ charge }), as Q450's close already does) instead of reading the event's charge.refunds. Guard to add: an edge case in src/test/edge/disputeSettlementWebhook.test.ts with refunds: null that still writes the ledger row. **STATUS 2026-10-04 (lead):** built on cloud/q1192-q1193; lh-money-escrow review CHANGES REQUIRED (Quick Release's pin broke on our own transfer.created webhook; the full-refund ledger row recorded the running total); both fixed by the lead (release pins escrow/payout_pending/released, the row records the refund's own amount), merged onto main's Q454 gift restore. Left: re-review, land, deploy, then the done-when.
- [ ] **Q1202 MEDIUM The Helpr on an unaccepted offer can push its deadline out and hold the job (lh-authz-rls review of Q1185, should-fix 4, 2026-10-03; predates Q1180).** enforce_helper_jobs_column_whitelist lets the assigned Helpr write jobs.response_deadline, so a Helpr on an unconfirmed offer can PATCH it years out and expire_unanswered_offers never fires: the poster waits on a Helpr who never accepts. Fix: the Helpr may only clear response_deadline (set it NULL, as the accept does), never move it; PGlite case red first.
- [ ] **Q1203 MEDIUM two edge functions compared a Louisiana date with the UTC date (found 2026-10-03).** `create-payment` (recurring_visit) refused a visit when `visit_date <= new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 10)` and measured the time left to the visit from UTC midnight; `charge-recurring-visits` had its own `todayUtc()`. From 19:00 CDT the UTC date is tomorrow's, so a visit's payment window closed the evening before it. FIXED: ONE helper, `louisianaToday()` (`supabase/functions/_shared/louisianaDate.ts`, America/Chicago; `accountPurge.ts`'s private copy now uses it), in both functions; create-payment also takes the visit's start from `jobLocalMidnightMs` instead of `T00:00:00Z`, so Checkout expires at the visit's Louisiana midnight. GUARD: `src/test/louisianaDateClass.test.ts` (no UTC date string in `supabase/functions` outside 6 listed exceptions with reasons, exact counts both ways; 3 @mutate lines killed) plus the Chicago-evening cases in `src/test/edge/create-payment.test.ts` and `charge-recurring-visits.test.ts` (red on main). create-payment is a money path: an lh-money-escrow review is required before landing. **STATUS 2026-10-04 (lead): landed in the 2026-10-04 batch 2, reviewed; tick after its deploy is verified live.**
- [ ] **Q1205 MEDIUM A poster can PATCH a job's direct-offer marker at any time (found by the lh-authz-rls review of the expired-offer change, 2026-10-03; read on prod, write not run).** authenticated holds UPDATE on jobs.direct_offer_status and jobs.direct_offer_expires_at; "Customers can update their own jobs" has no WITH CHECK, no CHECK bounds the columns, and neither enforce_poster_jobs_money_lock nor prevent_job_field_escalation names them, so a poster can re-arm 'pending' on a booked job (with a NULL expiry the sweep never clears it). The client no longer reads a stale marker as an offer (activityFilters isHeldOffer, d39666f53 follow-up), but the server should refuse it. Fix: only respond_to_direct_offer / the posting path / the sweeps write those columns (deny-list them for the poster after posting). GUARD when built: a PGlite probe (the poster's PATCH of either column is refused; the RPC and the sweep still write them).

Fix: a separate flag per direction and the withheld text kept out of the client's read (column grant or view).
- [~] **Q1207 LOW After the hourly sweep an expired offer reads "You weren't picked" (found by the same review, 2026-10-03).** expire_unanswered_offers sets the application to 'rejected' with no closed_reason, so deriveHelperWait turns "The offer expired" into "not_selected" within the hour. Fix: the sweep stamps closed_reason = 'offer_expired' and the status line reads it. FIXED 2026-10-04 (general lane), waits on db-deploy: migration 20261004184021_expired_offer_says_it_expired widens applications_closed_reason_check to admit 'offer_expired' and re-creates expire_unanswered_offers from the live body (md5(prosrc) 403c7428f71141d1361342cb17418467 = 20261004001807's) with the one close now `SET status = 'rejected', closed_reason = 'offer_expired'`; deriveHelperWait returns offer_expired for that stamp. No backfill: 0 rejected applications with a null closed_reason match a 'You lost a job offer' notice on prod (read-only SQL, 2026-10-04). PGlite: applied 3x, the sweep on an expired offer leaves rejected/offer_expired with 0 error_logs (prod's body leaves rejected/null). AppliedJobCard's hidden-job card says "Offer expired" for that stamp too (lh-silent-failure review should-fix). GUARD: src/test/expiredOfferAfterSweep.test.ts (3 of 5 red on the pre-fix tree) + the hidden-job case in src/components/job-card/activityBadgeListAgreement.test.tsx (red without the card change). Poster-side badge: Q1259. done-when: sql `SELECT (prosrc LIKE '%closed_reason = ''offer_expired''%')::text FROM pg_proc WHERE proname = 'expire_unanswered_offers'` => true
- [ ] **Q1194 LOW Query functions that read a Supabase result and never look at its `error` (found by the Q1182 lane, 2026-10-03).** Q1182's guard covers a query function that THROWS a Supabase error without unwrap(); it does not see one that never reads the error at all, which turns a refused read into a confident zero. Seen (read in source, not inventoried): the admin health fetcher (src/components/admin/adminHealth/useHealthData.ts) reads `.count`/`.data` from 7 results (email_send_log sent/dlq/suppressed, push_tokens total/ios/android/latest) and never reads any of their `.error`, so a failed read shows "0 sent" or "0 push tokens". Fix: unwrap() them (or report and say "unknown"); guard: extend src/test/helpers/queryFnReach.ts to flag a Supabase result reachable from a queryFn whose `error` is never read, and inventory the class first (count before fixing).
- [ ] **Q1195 LOW error_logs keeps only the message of an unwrap()'d Supabase error (lh-silent-failure review of Q1182, 2026-10-03).** src/lib/errorLogger.ts describeUnknownError returns `err.message` for any Error, and appends `code=`/`details=`/`hint=`/`status=` only for a plain object. unwrap() throws an Error copy carrying those fields, so every read reported through the query cache (about 200 unwrap() sites, plus the 40 Q1182 moved there) logs no PostgREST code, and a refused HEAD count logs an empty message (read in source; error_logs rows not checked). Fix: append those own properties for an Error too, after checking nothing (Sentry alert rules, throttle keys) matches the exact message format; guard: a test that reports an unwrap()'d 403 and asserts `code=42501` and `status=403` in the logged message.
- [ ] **Q1209 LOW The admin refunds do not check what the charge already refunded before refunding again (found by the lh-money-escrow review of Q454, 2026-10-03; code read only).** create-payment admin_refund_dispute and admin_refund_general call stripe.refunds.create with a fixed idempotency key (refund-dispute-<job>, refund-general-<job>-full) and never read the charge's amount_refunded first, as cancel_escrow does (it skips the call when latest_charge.amount_refunded already covers it). Inside Stripe's ~24h key window a retry replays the same refund; after it, a retry is refused by Stripe (the charge is already refunded), so a job whose flip failed after a refund can never be finished from the button (it pages and needs a hand). A double refund would need a capture of about 63 cents or less, below the $10 job minimum. Fix: read latest_charge.amount_refunded and reuse the existing refund (as cancel_escrow does). Guard to add: an edge case per action in src/test/edge/create-payment.test.ts.
- [ ] **Q1210 MEDIUM A job paid partly by a gift card and partly by card may never pay out (found by the lh-money-escrow review of Q454, 2026-10-03; code read only, NOT exercised: 0 mixed gift + card jobs on prod).** release-payout reads the PaymentIntent only when the job has NO redeemed gift (index.ts:536, `if (!giftCardRow)`), so on a mixed job escrowAmountReceivedCents stays null and the payout cap (escrowValueCents, :808) is the gift's applied value alone; a Helpr payout figured from the whole budget is then refused as 'exceeds captured escrow' whenever the card part is larger than the fee margin. The reviewer read the same shape in process-scheduled-payouts. Verify with an edge case (gift + PaymentIntent, both legs) before fixing; the fix is to read the PaymentIntent for a mixed job too and cap at captured + gift, as execute-dispute-split does.
- [ ] **Q1211 MEDIUM A refund or cancel that lands while a payout batch is running does not stop that batch's payout (found by the lh-money-escrow review of Q454, 2026-10-03; code read only, NOT exercised).** process-scheduled-payouts selects its due payout_pending jobs once (index.ts:81-84) and never re-reads payment_status before paying, and claimPayout (_shared/payoutClaim.ts:133) inserts its claim row without reading the job (prod has no trigger on payout_transfers). So a full admin refund (or charge.refunded) on a payout_pending job between the batch's read and its transfer refunds the card AND the Helpr is paid. Q454's gift restore reads the payout ledger after its flip, so it no longer adds a returned gift on top, but the card-plus-payout double spend stands. Fix: claimPayout (or the transfer step) re-reads the job under a row lock and refuses unless it is still payout_pending. Guard to add: a race proof (the CLAUDE.md race recipe) or an edge case asserting the re-read.
- [ ] **Q1212 LOW A gift card left unreturned after a refund closed its job is reported once and never again (found by the lh-money-escrow review of Q454, 2026-10-03).** Since Q454 the admin refunds return the gift AFTER the job's terminal flip; a restore that fails (or is refused because a payout moved) pages critical once and nothing re-reports it (Quick Refund's partial refund gets no charge.refunded retry). Add a money-reconciliation check: a gift_cards row 'redeemed' on a job now cancelled/refunded, with no replacement row (restored_from_job_id), no live payout_transfers row and no unexecuted decided dispute. Guard to add: an edge case in src/test/edge/money-reconciliation.test.ts.
- [ ] **Q1213 LOW A gift card that covers all but 1-49 cents of a job probably cannot finish posting (found by the lh-money-escrow review of Q454, 2026-10-03; code read only, NOT exercised).** redeem_gift_card puts no minimum on the shortfall (difference_cents, read live), and Stripe Checkout refuses a charge under 50 cents, so the shortfall checkout for such a job would fail. Verify with an edge case on the gift-card shortfall checkout in create-payment before fixing; the fix is to round a sub-50-cent shortfall into the gift's side or refuse that redemption with a clear message.
- [ ] **Q1208 LOW Q454 follow-ups from the lh-money-escrow final review (2026-10-03, code read only).** (1) returnGiftAfterRefund (create-payment) and chargeRefunded page "did not return the poster's gift card" whenever a payout row is pending/paid/reversed, without checking a gift exists: a full refund after a reversed transfer pages critical on a gift-less job (check for a redeemed/reserved gift first; give the payout-during-refund case its own page, a Q1211-class signal). (2) The general refund's payout check ignores 'failed' payout_transfers rows, which failClaim also writes for network faults where the transfer may exist at Stripe (Quick Refund asks Stripe first; the general refund does not). (3) When settle_dispute_by_external_refund answers 'closed' and the decision gave the Helpr a share, the whole gift still returns to the poster, and the admin notice asking whether to pay the Helpr does not say so. (4) The dispute record counts card + gift (create-payment ~2680) even when the replacement gift is unspendable (a revoked donation). Also two stale comments (~2641) and the settled branch returning without releasing refundClaimHeld (pre-existing).
- [ ] **Q1200 LOW Refuse an Apple in-app purchase from an unconfirmed-email session BEFORE the purchase sheet opens (found by the lh-authz-rls review of Q837, 2026-10-03).** verify-apple-iap is exempt from the Q837 gate on purpose: Apple has already charged when it runs, and it never refuses a paid purchase (grant and flag). The gate belongs at purchase time: live public.subscription_purchase_eligibility(text) (SECURITY DEFINER, STABLE), which the client calls before showing the sheet, does not check confirmation. Fix: return not-eligible (with a confirm-your-email reason) when public.session_email_unconfirmed(); the client already respects the eligibility answer. 0 unconfirmed accounts live (2026-10-03). GUARD when built: a PGlite probe (an unconfirmed session is not eligible, a confirmed one is) and a pin that the function reads session_email_unconfirmed.
- [ ] **Q1216 LOW reject_other_applications_on_accept has no caller since the accept fallback was retired (Q1187).** Its last caller was useOfferHandlers.ts's PGRST202 fallback; complete_job_accept closes the other applications itself. It is still SECURITY DEFINER and executable by authenticated (harmless as written: it refuses anyone but the confirmed Helpr and only rejects pending applications, which the accept already closed). Drop it, or REVOKE EXECUTE from authenticated, after checking for live callers (pg_stat_user_functions, edge functions, e2e).
- [ ] **Q1196 LOW src/test/hireColumnsRpcOnly.test.ts layer 3 reads `helper_id: null` as a hire write (found by the Q1187 lane, 2026-10-03).** Its FORBIDDEN pattern is /helper_id\s*:\s*(?!null\b)/: the second \s* backtracks to nothing and the lookahead then sees " null", so a client unassign written with a space is flagged (measured: true for "helper_id: null", false for "helper_id:null"). It errs toward red and no client jobs write unassigns today, so it has never fired. Fix: /helper_id\s*:(?!\s*null\b)/ and a case that pins it.
- [ ] **Q1214 LOW Accept-gate follow-ups (lh-authz-rls review of Q1187/Q1188, 2026-10-04, APPROVE with should-fixes).** (1) Commit the Q1185-first PGlite variant (reviewer's scratch _rv_q1185first.pglite.mjs: RV1-RV3, 28/28) as a mode of src/test/pglite/acceptGateHardening.pglite.mjs. (2) Restate claim_series_dates to set app.accept_rpc around its UPDATE, then drop the take-an-open-job exemption in enforce_helper_award_gate so the gate no longer leans on trg_hire_columns_rpc_only. (3) The sweep still locks job then profile; when the user's accept_job_offer is the deadlock victim they see a generic toast: log that side. (4) An offer that always fails stays accepted past its deadline and logs an error hourly with no escalation: count it in the ops ledger.
- [ ] **Q1230 MEDIUM (was Q1194 in its branch; renumbered on landing) A poster can read who viewed their job: job_views.viewer_id through PostgREST (found by the lh-authz-rls review of Q739, 2026-10-03; catalog evidence, no token-swap test yet).** Policy "Posters read views on own jobs" (SELECT on job_views where the caller owns the job) with viewer_id SELECT-granted hands the poster every viewer's user id, wider than the product, which shows counts only. Fix: serve the poster a count (an RPC or view) and drop the row-level policy, or revoke viewer_id from authenticated with the client's count query moved to a definer RPC. GUARD when built: a two-account PostgREST probe (the poster's select of job_views returns no viewer_id) plus a pin on the policy.
- [ ] **Q1231 MEDIUM (was Q1195 in its branch; renumbered on landing) A job party can forge job_revisions.requested_by or rewrite the poster's revision request (found by the lh-authz-rls review of Q739, 2026-10-03; catalog evidence).** "Job parties can manage revisions" is FOR ALL with a WITH CHECK that also admits the job's Helpr; authenticated holds INSERT and UPDATE on requested_by and description; the only triggers are the ban gate and the email gate; requested_by's FK is ON DELETE SET NULL. Fix: split the policy (the poster inserts their own request; the Helpr may only answer via helper_response/status through an RPC) and a BEFORE trigger pinning requested_by := auth.uid() on a client INSERT and refusing its change on UPDATE. GUARD when built: a PGlite probe (the Helpr cannot insert as the poster nor rewrite the description) and the column-scoped grant check.
- [ ] **Q1232 LOW (was Q1196 in its branch; renumbered on landing) applications.flag_reason (a moderation internal) is exported to the applicant and readable by the poster (found by the lh-authz-rls review of Q739, 2026-10-03).** export_my_data strips messages.flag_reason but returns applications rows whole to the applicant, and the poster's SELECT policy on applications exposes the column. Fix: strip flag_reason (and flagged_hidden if it is moderation-only) from the applicant's export, add it to EXEMPT as stripped, and revoke SELECT (flag_reason) from authenticated after checking the client reads it nowhere. GUARD when built: the existing stripped-columns check (EXEMPT stripped entry) and a column-grant pin.
- [ ] **Q1233 LOW (was Q1197 in its branch; renumbered on landing) The export guard's policy replay does not equal the live policies (found by the lh-authz-rls review of Q739, 2026-10-03).** Live policy "disputes parties select" on public.disputes is in no migration file and no schema_migrations statement (made outside the migrations), while the replay still carries "disputes job parties select", which is not live; the replay now reads bare names and ALTER POLICY RENAME/USING, but nothing compares it with the live set. Fix: a migration that restates the disputes SELECT policy as live (so the files describe prod again), then a two-way name check of the replayed SELECT/ALL policies against the write-contract snapshot's live policy list, and a failure for any SELECT/ALL policy that calls a function outside a known list. GUARD when built: that two-way check in src/test/dataExportCoversEveryUserTable.test.ts.
- [ ] **Q1234 MEDIUM (was Q1198 in its branch; renumbered on landing) applications column ownership is not enforced: each party can write the other's fields (found by the lh-authz-rls re-review of Q739, 2026-10-03; catalog evidence, no write test).** lock_applications_owner_columns pins only helper_id and job_id, and authenticated holds INSERT and UPDATE on every applications column: the applicant's INSERT and pending-UPDATE policies let them set the poster's offer_message, decline_reason, poster_viewed_at and closed_reason; "Job owners can update application status" lets the poster rewrite the applicant's message, attachment_urls, stake_* and flag_*. No money impact today (no function reads stake_*; 0 of 49 rows carry a stake). Fix: a BEFORE UPDATE/INSERT trigger (or column grants) splitting the columns by party, with the accept/decline RPCs as the poster's writers. GUARD when built: a PGlite probe (each party's write to the other's columns is refused) plus a column-ownership pin.
- [ ] **Q1235 LOW (was Q1199 in its branch; renumbered on landing) Download My Data has no never-wider check for the Helpr's side (found by the lh-authz-rls re-review of Q739, 2026-10-03).** The caller-scope check accepts any `(SELECT j.id FROM public.jobs j WHERE j.helper_id = v_uid)` sub-select, so adding it to e.g. payment_refunds (the poster's refunds) stays green while it hands the Helpr rows their RLS never shows them; Q739 built the poster-side check only. - [ ] **Q1221 MEDIUM A payout hold does not pause Stripe's automatic payouts from a held Helpr's Connect balance to their bank (found building Q764, 2026-10-03).** Q764's hold stops every platform-to-Connect transfer and instant payouts, but money already transferred to the Helpr's Connect account still leaves on Stripe's own payout schedule. Pausing that needs a Connect account settings change (payouts schedule to manual while held, restored on release) or a decision that the transfer stop is enough. OWNER DECISION. Done when the owner has chosen, and, if paused, admin_set/release_payout_hold drive it with a test that fails without it.
- [ ] **Q1222 MEDIUM A tip Checkout opened before a payout hold and paid after it still pays the held Helpr (lh-money-escrow review of Q764, 2026-10-04; code read only).** create-payment's tip is a destination charge (transfer_data at ~create-payment:1348): the hold is checked when the session is created, and Stripe moves the money to the Helpr whenever the poster pays, with no second check. Done when a session paid while the Helpr is held does not reach them (e.g. expire open tip sessions on hold, or switch tips to separate charges and transfers), with a test that fails without it.
- [ ] **Q1223 MEDIUM A won chargeback's re-payment to a held Helpr is never re-run after the hold is released (lh-money-escrow review of Q764, 2026-10-04).** stripe-webhook/handlers/_chargebackClawback.ts repayClawback skips a held Helpr (the row stays 'reversed' and pages "re-pay FAILED"); chargeDisputeClosed.ts (~:239) is the only caller and the event does not recur, so release of the hold never pays it back automatically. Done when releasing a hold re-drives owed clawback re-payments (or a sweep does), with a test that fails without it.
- [ ] **Q1224 LOW A long payout hold silently drops a poster's auto-tip (lh-money-escrow review of Q764, 2026-10-04).** auto-tip-charge skips a held Helpr's tip each tick without claiming it; auto_tip_candidates only offers a job for 14 days, so a hold longer than that means the tip is never charged and nobody is told. Done when an auto-tip that ages out under a hold is recorded and the poster is told (or the window extends while held), with a test.
- [ ] **Q1240 MEDIUM money-reconciliation exempts a WHOLE crew job from payout_pending_stranded when any one member is held (lh-money-escrow re-review of Q764, 2026-10-04).** An unheld member B whose leg stops at no_connect_account / no_pi / an orphaned already_claimed is then silent for as long as A's hold lasts (B's hard transfer failures still page through jobDefect). Fix: exempt only when every UNPAID member is held (payout_transfers is already scanned in the same run; move that scan above the exemption). done-when: a test with a held member and a stranded unheld member pages, with @mutate.
- [ ] **Q1241 MEDIUM void-cancelled-payments warns #ops-alerts every run while a held Helpr's cancellation fee waits (lh-money-escrow re-review of Q764, 2026-10-04).** It claims the cancellation_fee_transfers row as pending before it checks the hold, so cancellation_fee_transfer_not_paid posts a warning on every run for the length of the hold (not a cron defect; warnings are excluded). Same class as Q764's closed must-fix. Fix: check the hold before the claim, or exempt held Helprs from that warning. done-when: a held Helpr's pending fee posts no warning, with @mutate. Unreachable until the first hold is placed.
- [ ] **Q1242 MEDIUM A person cannot delete a message the other party replied to (found while fixing Q1166, 2026-10-03; pre-existing; reproduced in PGlite on prod's bodies, not on prod).** messages_reply_to_id_fkey is ON DELETE SET NULL, and that cascade UPDATEs the reply while the deleter's request is still in place (auth.uid() = the deleter), so the BEFORE UPDATE triggers treat it as a client write: enforce_message_non_sender_read_only sees a non-sender changing reply_to_id and raises "a message may only be edited by the person who sent it", which fails the whole DELETE (the client shows "Couldn't delete that one"). Deleting a message nobody replied to, or one only you replied to, works. Live 2026-10-03: 1 reply, cross-party, so 1 message its sender cannot delete today; 0 error_logs rows with that text. Same result before and after 20261004001242 in PGlite. Fix direction: let the trigger accept reply_to_id going to NULL when the parent it named no longer exists (the FK's own cascade, which reaches it at pg_trigger_depth() > 1), never a client's own PATCH of reply_to_id; needs an lh-authz-rls review. GUARD when built: a PGlite probe (the sender deletes a message the receiver replied to: 1 row; the reply survives with reply_to_id NULL; a client still cannot clear reply_to_id on someone else's message).
