NEW_MIGRATION=skip crashes: no migration before 20260923123701 defines is_off_job, which the newest gate it loads needs. The CI half (scripts/ci/null-arg-validators.sql, run on the real replay in db-smoke) is not affected; only this local proof rotted. Fix: take the count from the SQL, add email_confirmed_at to the fixture, and load skip mode's helpers from the newest definition that exists before the fix. GUARD when built: the proof's own self-checks green in both modes (it is not in CI: pglite is not a dependency).
- [ ] **Q1244 MEDIUM A party can open and withdraw a dispute in a loop, and each filing pages ops and notifies people (should-fix from the lh-authz-rls re-review of Q1169, 2026-10-04; filed, not built).** rpc_open_dispute and rpc_withdraw_dispute are EXECUTE-able by authenticated (read live) and flip a job between disputed and its prior status as often as a party likes. 20261004001242 brakes the in-thread status notices (latest-notice check, 6 per participant per 10 minutes), but per the review each filing after a withdrawal also inserts a disputes row, pages Slack as critical through notify_ops_dispute_filed (called from open_dispute_as, read live; the review says it has no dedupe, not re-measured here) and notifies the other party and the admins. open_dispute_as has no cooldown: the only interval in it (read live) is the 30-day velocity count that writes a high_dispute_rate fraud flag and refuses nothing. Suggested fix (the review): in open_dispute_as, refuse a new dispute from the same opener on the same job within about 10 minutes of one they withdrew; needs an lh-money-escrow review (dispute state holds escrow). GUARD when built: a PGlite probe (open, withdraw, open again inside the window: refused with a clear code; after the window: allowed) and a pin on the cooldown predicate. In the thread itself (round-3 review, 2026-10-04): with Q1169's cap a sustained open/withdraw loop still sends up to 6 notices per participant per job per 10 minutes, about 36 pushes an hour; a cooldown on open_dispute_as also bounds that.
- [ ] **Q1247 MEDIUM Recurring-visit payer is told the wrong thing, or nothing, about an on-session visit payment that cannot be booked (found by Q750 and its lh-money-escrow review, 2026-10-03; read, not reproduced in the UI).** (a) supabase/functions/stripe-webhook/handlers/settleRecurringVisitCheckout.ts refunds an unbookable on-session payment (no row, row not pending, series over, amount/currency/payer mismatch) and pages ops, but writes no notification to the payer: they see the money come back with no word from us. (b) src/pages/posts/postedJobs/RecurringVisitPayments.tsx lists every `pending` row dated after today; a row of a series that just ended stays pending until charge-recurring-visits' next daily sweep expires it (Q750 (4)), so the card offers "Pay $X" and create-payment refuses with "This series has ended". (c) The sweep (charge-recurring-visits) flips a pending row to expired BEFORE `checkout.sessions.expire`; a Checkout completed between the two is refunded in full by the webhook (the row is no longer pending), but the payer was already sent "you weren't charged". Fix (b) at the source: expire a series' pending rows in the same transaction that ends it (end_recurring_series, end_series_for_banned_account, the parent cancel) or filter the card on the parent's series_ended_on/status; fix (c) by closing the Checkout first, or wording the notice for the race. GUARD when built: an edge test that every refund branch of settleRecurringVisitCheckout writes the payer a notification, a PGlite case that ending a series leaves none of its rows pending, and an edge test that a Checkout completed mid-sweep never yields "you weren't charged".
- [ ] **Q1248 LOW A retried recurring-visit webhook posts a second "paid but not bookable — refunded" warning (lh-money-escrow review of Q750, 2026-10-03; read, not reproduced).** settleRecurringVisitCheckout counts a refund as already done only when Stripe REFUSES it (charge_already_refunded or an idempotency conflict). Within the 24h key life Stripe replays the first refund as a success, so a redelivered event (for example after the expire write threw) refunds nothing new but posts the ops warning again. Money is right; ops sees the same refund twice. Fix: compare the replayed refund's creation time, or list the intent's refunds first and skip the warning when one predates this delivery. GUARD when built: an edge test that two deliveries of one refunded payment post exactly one warning.

Decide per CLAUDE.md's seed rules whether seed rows are swept (they must still settle) but routed to the seed digest, and say which. GUARD when built: an edge test that a seed series' sweep alerts carry the seed marker (scripts and src/test/seedNeverNotifiesReal.test.ts are the pattern).

(2) Up to 1,000 rows a run (500 stale + 500 over), each paid one 3-4 Stripe calls, could approach the edge function's time limit; low at today's volume (0 rows on prod). (3) A paid row of a CANCELLED parent with series_ended_on NULL is refunded in full (fee not withheld, unlike an ended series under Q808), and the webhook treats a missing parent as over and refunds in full while the cron leaves it alone: neither is a wrong refund; decide whether they should match (ties to Q1223's owner decision).
